       + 3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv\n                         - gamma * computeLaplacian(c, nx, ny, nz, dx, dy, dz, x, y, z);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Cahn-Hilliard update step\nvoid cahnHilliardUpdate(std::vector<double>& cnew, const std::vector<double>& cold,\n                        const std::vector<double>& mu,\n                        const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const x;\n                const double pseudo = ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n                c[idx] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& nx, ny)] - 2.0 * fc) * inv_dx2;\n    const double fyy = (f[idx3(x, yp, zh, nx, ny)] + f[idx3(x, yn, zh, nx, ny)] - 2.0 * fc) * inv_dy2;\n    const double fzz = (f[idx3(x, y, zh + 1, nx, ny)] + z<nz; ++z) for (size_t y=0; y<ny; ++y) for (size_t x=0; x<nx; ++x) {\n            const size_t idx = idx3(x,y,z,nx,ny);\n            cnew[idx] = cold[idx] + dt*D*computeLaplacian(mu,nx,ny,nz,dx,dy,dz,x,y,z);\n        }\n        std::swap(cold,cnew);\n    }\n    print_results(cold, \"Concentration\");\n    return 0;\n}\nEOF\ncp /home/llmtest/evals/20260901-162328/cahn-hilliard_claude-fable-5-cc-medium_hybrid_r2/common/results_output.hpp /home/llmtest/evals/20260901-162328/cahn-hilliard_claude-fable-5-cc-medium_hybrid_r2/common/hash.hpp . 2>/dev/null; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011CeirH75aPV4KhggK335t9","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. The Cahn–Hilliard benchmark is now a hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA code, bit-exact against the serial reference and roughly **75× faster** (about 160 MCellUpdates/s serial vs 12,309 MCellUpdates/s on 4 GPUs at 384³).\n\n## How it's parallelized\n\n- **MPI**: 1D slab decomposition along Z with remainder planes given to the lowest ranks. Each rank binds to a GPU via its node-local rank (`MPI_Comm_split_type` with `MPI_COMM_TYPE_SHARED`), so the same binary scales across multi-GPU nodes on a cluster. One Z-plane halo is exchanged per field (concentration, then chemical potential) each timestep through pinned host staging buffers with `MPI_Sendrecv`. Ranks beyond the plane count stay safely idle, so any rank count works.\n- **CUDA**: both stencil sweeps (chemical potential and the concentration update) run as 3D-grid kernels on device-resident arrays with a device pointer swap between steps — the field never round-trips through the host during the run. The clamped boundary condition is reproduced exactly by filling the halo plane at global domain edges with a device-to-device copy of the adjacent interior plane, so the kernels have no Z-branching.\n- **OpenMP**: parallelizes the host-side work — decomposition-independent field initialization (same global pseudo-random sequence as the original) and the NaN/range validation reduction on rank 0.\n\n## Correctness\n\nResults are gathered to rank 0 with `MPI_Gatherv` for `-r`/`-v`, so output format and validation semantics are unchanged. Since `dx=dy=dz=1.0`, the reciprocal-multiply refactor is bit-identical to the original divisions, and `-fmad=false` pins the GPU to strict source-order IEEE arithmetic. The FNV-1a result hash matches the serial code exactly for every configuration I tested (64³/20 steps, 96×48×80/15 steps, 384³/20 steps) on 1–5 ranks, including rank counts that don't divide the grid. One caveat worth knowing: the original CMake's `-O3 -march=native` lets g++ contract multiply-adds into FMAs, which itself perturbs the last bits — the parallel code matches the well-defined uncontracted semantics (g++ with `-ffp-contract=off` agrees bit-for-bit).\n\n## Scaling (384³, 20 steps)\n\n1 GPU: 5,606 → 2 GPUs: 8,917 → 4 GPUs: 12,309 MCellUpdates/s. Sub-linear at 4 GPUs because two of the 3090 pairs share PCIe bandwidth for halo traffic and the per-GPU slab shrinks; scaling improves with larger grids, which is the regime a cluster run targets.\n\nOnly the two existing files were changed: `cahn_hilliard.cpp` (compiled as CUDA via `set_source_files_properties`, keeping the single-file layout) and `CMakeLists.txt` (enables the CUDA language with native arch detection, finds MPI/OpenMP/CUDAToolkit, links via the host C++ compiler). The executable is still `cahn_hilliard`; run it as `mpirun -np <gpus> ./cahn_hilliard ...`."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":504,"cache_read_input_tokens":42119,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":504},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"a0b3f21f-1fb9-4224-a7e3-75117f5fe4e0","uuid":"e109ffc2-5cd5-46f1-9697-998575751515","timestamp":"2026-09-04T18:11:50.691Z","request_id":"req_011CeirH533bsa5MUaTXDceY"}
